&=&\llparenthesis(\mathsf{skip}\,\&\,t\,;p_{1}\,;{(t\,;p_{1})}^{\langle n\rangle})\,;\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\mathsf{skip}\,;\neg t\,\&\,{t\,;p_{1}\,;{(t\,;p_{1})}^{\langle n\rangle}}\,;\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Corollary 1]})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\neg t\,\&\,{t\,;p_{1}\,;{(t\,;p_{1})}^{\langle n\rangle}}\,;\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(\cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Lemma 4]})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\neg t\,\&\,t\,;p_{1}\,;{p_{2}}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(IH for $n$)}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\neg t\,\&\,t\,;p_{2}\,;{p_{2}}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(Assumption for $p_{1}$)}\\
&=&\llparenthesis t\,;p_{2}\,;{p_{2}}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\,\&\,\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Lemma 4]})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis t\,;p_{2}\,;{p_{2}}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\,\&\,\neg t\,;\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Lemma 4]})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p_{2}\,;{p_{2}}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\,\mathsf{else}\,\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\llparenthesis{p_{2}}^{\langle n+1,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

∎

Lemma 0 (Equivalence of alternate definition of approximation in
ProbNetKAT
).

Let
\langle p\rangle_{n}
denote the
n
-th approximant
for a policy
p
as originally defined in
Smolka et al. 2017
. The alternate
definition for approximation is equivalent in the following sense:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\colon&\llparenthesis\langle p\rangle_{n}\rrparenthesis&=&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}\end{array}

Proof.

By structural induction on
p
. All cases follow immediately except for
guarded iteration, as the definitions coincide exactly. We consider only
this case.

Case
p=\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p
:

We unfold the LHS/RHS first:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis\langle\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p\rangle_{n}\rrparenthesis&=&\llparenthesis\langle(t\,;p)^{*}\,;\neg t\rangle_{n}\rrparenthesis\\
&=&\llparenthesis{(\langle t\,;p\rangle_{n})}^{\langle n\rangle}\,;\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}]})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis{(t\,;\langle p\rangle_{n})}^{\langle n\rangle}\,;\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}]})}\\
\\
\llparenthesis\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p\rrparenthesis_{n}&=&\llparenthesis\left(\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p\right)_{n}\rrparenthesis\\
&=&\llparenthesis{(\left(p\right)_{n})}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(Alternate definition)}\end{array}

Finally, we conclude:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis{(t\,;\langle p\rangle_{n})}^{\langle n\rangle}\,;\neg t\rrparenthesis&=&\llparenthesis{(\left(p\right)_{n})}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lmm:alt-defn-guarded-iteration-equiv-probnetkat} and IH)}\end{array}

∎

Definition 0 (Subsingleton support of a discrete measure).

We say a discrete measure
\mu\in\mathcal{D}(2^{\mathsf{H}})
has a

subsingleton support
if
\quad\forall\{a\}\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)\colon|a|\leq 1
.

Definition 0 (Subsingleton support for Markov kernels).

We say that a (discrete) Markov kernel
P\colon 2^{\mathsf{H}}\times\mathcal{B}\rightarrow[0,1]
has a

subsingleton support
if
\quad\forall a\ \text{s.t.}\ |a|\leq 1\colon P(a,-)\ \text{has subsingleton support}
.

Lemma 0 (Bind preserves subsingleton support).

For any discrete measure
\mu\in\mathcal{D}(2^{\mathsf{H}})
and
Markov kernel
P\colon 2^{\mathsf{H}}\times\mathcal{B}\rightarrow[0,1]

with subsingleton support, we have that
\mu\gg\!=P
has
subsingleton support.

Proof.

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&\mu\gg\!=P\\
&=&\lambda A.\int_{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}}P(a,A)\cdot\mu(da)&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Figure 2]})}\\
\\
&=&\lambda A.\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}P(a,\{b\})\cdot\mu(\{a\})&A=\{b\}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}&\text{($\mu$ is discrete)}\\
\end{array}

Consider any
a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}
. By assumption
for
\mu
, we have that
|a|\leq 1
. Therefore, for any

b\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}
such
that
\{b\}\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu\gg\!=P)
,
we have by assumption for
P
that
|b|\leq 1
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Subsingleton support of conditional choice).

For any *-free policies
p
,
q
and predicate
t
in
ProbNetKAT
: if
\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis
and
\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis
have subsingleton support then

\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q\rrparenthesis\ \text{has subsingleton support}.

Proof.

We proceed by case analysis
on
\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(a)
. There are two cases (
[
Smolka et al. 2017
, Lemma 2]
), from both of
which we determine that either

\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)=\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)
or

\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)=\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)
.
In either case, the property holds by assumption.
∎

Lemma 0 (Subsingleton support of guarded iteration).

For any *-free policy
p
and predicate
t
in
ProbNetKAT
:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\colon&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis\ \text{has subsingleton support}&\Rightarrow&\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis\ \text{has subsingleton support}\end{array}

Proof.

By induction on
n
.

Case
n=0
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle 0,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&=&\llparenthesis\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

The property holds for predicates as they behave like packet filters

[
Smolka et al. 2017
, Lemma 2]
.

Case
n=n+1
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n+1,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&=&\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\,\mathsf{else}\,\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

By
Lemma

11
, we have to show
that both
\llparenthesis p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis

and
\llparenthesis\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis
have subsingleton support. Any atomic
program only acts on individual histories
[
Smolka et al. 2017
, Lemma 3]
,
so we have that
\llparenthesis\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis
has subsingleton support. We
proceed to show that

\llparenthesis p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis

has subsingleton support.
We have:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis(a)&=&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis(a)\gg\!=\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Figure 2]})}\end{array}

By
Lemma

10
, we need to show that

\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis(a)
and
\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis

have subsingleton support. However, these conditions hold by
assumption for
p
and by the induction hypothesis for
n
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Subsingleton support of approximants of guarded
ProbNetKAT
policies).

For any policy
p
in the guarded fragment of
ProbNetKAT
,

\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}
has subsingleton support.

Proof.

By structural induction on the policy
p
. For predicates
t
this follows from
them behaving like packet filters
[
Smolka et al. 2017
, Lemma 2]
. Likewise, the property holds
for atomic programs as they only act on individual histories
[
Smolka et al. 2017
, Lemma 3]
.
We consider only the remaining cases:

Case
p=p\,;q
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis p\,;q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)&=&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)\gg\!=\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis_{n}&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Figure 2]})}\end{array}

Therefore, the property holds by
Lemma

10

and IH.

Case
p=\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q
:
By
Lemma

11
and IH.

Case
p=\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)&=&\llparenthesis\left(\mathsf{while}\,t\,\mathsf{do}\,p\right)_{n}\rrparenthesis(a)&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}]})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis{(\left(p\right)_{n})}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis(a)&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lmm:probnetkat-alt-approx-pol-equiv})}\end{array}

Therefore, the property holds by
Lemma

12

and IH.

Case
p=p\oplus_{r}q
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llparenthesis p\oplus_{r}q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)&=&\llparenthesis\left(p\right)_{n}\oplus_{r}\left(q\right)_{n}\rrparenthesis(a)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&r\cdot\llparenthesis{\left(p\right)_{n}}\rrparenthesis(a)+(1-r)\cdot\llparenthesis{\left(q\right)_{n}}\rrparenthesis(a)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&r\cdot\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)+(1-r)\cdot\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a)&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

Therefore, we have that

\mathsf{supp}(\llparenthesis p\oplus_{r}q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a))\subseteq\mathsf{supp}(\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}(a))\cup\mathsf{supp}(\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis_{n}(a))

and the property holds by IH.
∎

Note that approximants are *-free programs so their probability distributions
are discrete.

Lemma 0 (Equivalence of unit across
\mathsf{Prob}
-
wNetKAT
and
guarded
ProbNetKAT
).

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\forall h\in\mathsf{H}\colon&\eta(h)\sim\delta_{\{h\}}\end{array}

Proof.

We prove the three conditions of
Definition

4
separately:

•

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\ |\ 1<|a|\}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\{h\}}(a)=0
holds
by definition.

•

|\eta(h)|=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\{h\}}(\{\{h\}\})

|\eta(h)|=1=\delta_{\{h\}}(\{\{h\}\})=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\{h\}}(\{\{h\}\})

•

\forall h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\colon\eta(h)(h^{\prime})=\delta_{\{h\}}(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})

We proceed by case analysis:

–

h=h^{\prime}
:

\eta(h)(h)=1=\delta_{\{h\}}(\{\{h\}\})

–

h\neq h^{\prime}
:

\eta(h)(h^{\prime})=0=\delta_{\{h\}}(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})

∎

Definition 0.

We lift equivalence between weightings and discrete measures to functions
and Markov kernels as follows.

\begin{array}[]{llclrcl}\forall f\colon\mathsf{H}\rightarrow\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathsf{Real}}(\mathsf{H}),P\colon 2^{\mathsf{H}}\times\mathcal{B}\rightarrow[0,1]\colon&f\sim P&:\Longleftrightarrow&\forall h\colon&f(h)\sim P(\{h\},-)\end{array}

Lemma 0 (Bind respects equivalence across
\mathsf{Prob}
-
wNetKAT

and guarded
ProbNetKAT
).

Given a weighting
m\in\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathsf{Real}}(\mathsf{H})
, a discrete
measure
\mu\in\mathcal{D}(2^{\mathsf{H}})
, a function

f\colon\mathsf{H}\rightarrow\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathsf{Real}}(\mathsf{H})
, and
a Markov kernel
P\colon 2^{\mathsf{H}}\times\mathcal{B}\rightarrow[0,1]
,
we have:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}m\sim\mu\wedge f\sim P&\Rightarrow&m\gg\!=f\sim\mu\gg\!=P\end{array}

Proof.

We begin by unfolding the RHS:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&\mu\gg\!=P\\
&=&\lambda A.\,\int_{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}}P(a,A)\cdot\mu(da)&\text{(By definition \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{cantor}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}]})}\\
&=&\lambda A.\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}P(a,\{b\})\cdot\mu(\{a\})&A=\{b\}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}&\text{($\mu$ is discrete)}\\
&=&\lambda A.\begin{cases}P(\varnothing,\{b\})\cdot\mu(\{\varnothing\})+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}P(\{h\},\{b\})\cdot\mu(\{\{h\}\})&A=\{b\}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}&\text{($m\sim\mu$)}\end{array}

We now prove all three conditions of
Definition

4
separately.

•

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\ |\ 1<|a|\}\end{subarray}}(\mu\gg\!=P)(\{a\})=0
follows immediately from the property holding for
P
.

•

|m\gg\!=f|=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}(\mu\gg\!=P)(\{\{h\}\})

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&|m\gg\!=f|\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m\gg\!=f)\end{subarray}}(m\gg\!=f)(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m\gg\!=f)\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot f(h^{\prime})(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m\gg\!=f)\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot f(h^{\prime})(h)&\text{(associativity of countable summation)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(f(h^{\prime}))\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot f(h^{\prime})(h)&\text{(*)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(f(h^{\prime}))\end{subarray}}f(h^{\prime})(h)&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:omega-comp-semirings})}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})&\text{($f\sim P$)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})&\text{($m\sim\mu$)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})&\text{($f\sim P$)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})\cdot P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:omega-comp-semirings})}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})\cdot P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})&\text{(associativity of countable summation)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})\cdot\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}(\mu\gg\!=P)(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

* This equation is due to the following:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&h\in\mathsf{supp}(m\gg\!=f)\\
&\iff&h\in\mathsf{supp}\left(\lambda h.\,\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot f(h^{\prime})(h)\right)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&\iff&\mathsf{supp}(m)\neq\varnothing\wedge h\in\mathsf{supp}(f(h^{\prime}))&\text{(Simple arithmetic)}\end{array}

Furthermore, as we are considering an expression of the form:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)}\end{subarray}}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(m\gg\!=f)}\end{subarray}}{...}}

We can rewrite it as done above.

•

\forall h\in\mathsf{supp}(m\gg\!=f)\colon(m\gg\!=f)(h)=(\mu\gg\!=P)(\{\{h\}\})

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&(\mu\gg\!=P)(\{\{h\}\})\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}P(\{h^{\prime}\},\{\{h\}\})\cdot\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}f(h^{\prime})(h)\cdot\mu(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})&\text{($f\sim P$)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}f(h^{\prime})(h)\cdot m(h^{\prime})&\text{($m\sim\mu$)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}f(h^{\prime})(h)\cdot m(h^{\prime})&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}m(h^{\prime})\cdot f(h^{\prime})(h)&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&(m\gg\!=f)(h)\end{array}

∎

Lemma 0 (Scalar multiplication respects equivalence).

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\forall m\in\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathsf{Real}}(\mathsf{H}),\mu\in\mathcal{D}(2^{\mathsf{H}})\colon&m\sim\mu&\Rightarrow&r\cdot m\sim r\cdot\mu\end{array}

Proof.

We prove all three conditions of
Definition

4
separately.

•

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\ |\ 1<|a|\}\end{subarray}}(r\cdot\mu)(\{a\})=0
follows immediately from the property holding for
\mu
.

•

|r\cdot m|=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}(r\cdot\mu)(\{\{h\}\})

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&|r\cdot m|\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(r\cdot m)\end{subarray}}(r\cdot m)(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(r\cdot m)\end{subarray}}r\cdot m(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}r\cdot m(h)&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&r\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m)\end{subarray}}m(h)&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&r\cdot\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(Assumption)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}r\cdot\mu(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}(r\cdot\mu)(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

•

\forall h\in\mathsf{supp}(r\cdot m)\colon(r\cdot m)(h)=(r\cdot\mu)(\{\{h\}\})

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&(r\cdot m)(h)\\
&=&r\cdot m(h)&\text{(By definiton)}\\
&=&r\cdot\mu(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(Assumption*)}\\
&=&(r\cdot\mu)(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

* Note that
h\in\mathsf{supp}(r\cdot m)\Rightarrow h\in\mathsf{supp}(m)
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Addition respects equivalence across
\mathsf{Prob}
-
wNetKAT

and guarded
ProbNetKAT
).

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\forall m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathsf{Real}}(\mathsf{H}),\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{D}(2^{\mathsf{H}})\colon&m_{1}\sim\mu_{1}\wedge m_{2}\sim\mu_{2}&\Rightarrow&m_{1}+m_{2}\sim\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}\end{array}

Proof.

We prove all three conditions of
Definition

4
separately.

•

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\ |\ 1<|a|\}\end{subarray}}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})(\{a\})=0
follows immediately from the property holding for
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
.

•

|m_{1}+m_{2}|=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})(\{\{h\}\})

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&|m_{1}+m_{2}|\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{1}+m_{2})\end{subarray}}(m_{1}+m_{2})(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{1}+m_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{1}(h)+m_{2}(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{1}+m_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{1}(h)\right)+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{1}+m_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{2}(h)&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{1})\end{subarray}}m_{1}(h)\right)+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{2}(h)&\text{($\mathsf{supp}(m_{i}(h))\subseteq\mathsf{supp}(m_{1}+m_{2})$)}\\
&=&\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu_{1}(\{\{h\}\})\right)+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{2}(h)&\text{(Assumption)}\\
&=&\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu_{1}(\{\{h\}\})\right)+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu_{2}(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(Assumption)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\mu_{1}(\{\{h\}\})+\mu_{2}(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\\
\end{array}

•

\forall h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{1}+m_{2})\colon(m_{1}+m_{2})(h)=(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})(\{\{h\}\})

\begin{array}[]{rclr}&&(m_{1}+m_{2})(h)\\
&=&m_{1}(h)+m_{2}(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\mu_{1}(\{\{h\}\})+m_{2}(h)&\text{(Assumption*)}\\
&=&\mu_{1}(\{\{h\}\})+\mu_{2}(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(Assumption*)}\\
&=&(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})(\{\{h\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\\
\end{array}

* We have
h\in\mathsf{supp}(m_{i})
or
m_{i}(h)=0
,
in either case we have
m_{i}(h)=\mu_{i}(\{\{h\}\})

by assumption.

∎

Lemma 0 (Predicate equivalence across
\mathsf{Prob}
-
wNetKAT
and
guarded
ProbNetKAT
).

For any predicate
t
in
ProbNetKAT
, we have

\forall h\in\mathsf{H}\colon\llbracket t\rrbracket\sim\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis

Proof.

By structural induction on the predicate
t
. For all cases, we
have that

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{a\in 2^{\mathsf{H}}\ |\ 1<|a|\}\end{subarray}}\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(h)(\{a\})=0

by
Lemma

13
. We prove only the remaining properties
of
Definition

4
.

Case
t=\mathsf{false}
:

We need only show that

|\llbracket\mathsf{false}\rrbracket(h)|=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\llparenthesis\mathsf{false}\rrparenthesis(h)(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})
.
We have:

|\llbracket\mathsf{false}\rrbracket(h)|=0=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\varnothing}(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\llparenthesis\mathsf{false}\rrparenthesis(h)(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})

Case
t=\mathsf{true}
:
By
Lemma

14
.

Case
t=f=n
:

Let
h=\pi{::}h
. We assume
\pi.f=n
, otherwise
the same reasoning as in the
\mathsf{false}
case applies.

(1)

|\llbracket f=n\rrbracket(\pi{::}h)|=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\llparenthesis f=n\rrparenthesis(\pi{::}h)(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}|\llbracket f=n\rrbracket(\pi{::}h)|&=&\eta(\pi{::}h)(\pi{::}h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\delta_{\{\pi{::}h\}}(\{\pi{::}h\})&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lmm:equiv-eta-dirac})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis f=n\rrparenthesis(\{\pi{::}h\})(\{\{\pi{::}h\}\})&\text{(By definition)}\\
&=&\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}\end{subarray}}\llparenthesis f=n\rrparenthesis(\{\pi{::}h\})(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})&\text{(Standard arithmetic)}\end{array}

(2)

\forall h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket f=n\rrbracket(h))\colon\llbracket f=n\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\llparenthesis f=n\rrparenthesis(\{h\})(\{\{h^{\prime}\}\})

Let
h=\pi{::}h
. Because

h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket f=n\rrbracket(h))
,
we have that
h^{\prime}=\pi{::}h
and that

\pi.f=n
. Therefore,

\llbracket f=n\rrbracket(\pi{::}h)(\pi{::}h)=1=\llparenthesis f=n\rrparenthesis(\{\pi{::}h\})(\{\{\pi{::}h\}\})

Case
t=t\,\vee\,u
:

We proceed by a further case analysis on
\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})
. We have the
following two cases (
Smolka et al. 2017
):

(1)

\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\varnothing}
(i.e. the case “false”)

By IH for
t
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)=0
. We have:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket t\,\vee\,u\rrbracket(h)&=&\llbracket u\rrbracket(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
\\
\llparenthesis t\,\vee\,u\rrparenthesis(\{h\})&=&\llparenthesis u\rrparenthesis(\{h\})&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Corollary 1]})}\end{array}

Finally, we have

\llbracket u\rrbracket(h)\sim\llparenthesis u\rrparenthesis(\{h\})

by IH for
u
.

(2)

\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\{h\}}
(i.e. the case “true”)

By IH for
t
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)=\eta(h)
. We have:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket t\,\vee\,u\rrbracket(h)&=&\eta(h)&\text{(By definition)}\\
\\
\llparenthesis t\,\vee\,u\rrparenthesis(\{h\})&=&\delta_{\{h\}}&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Corollary 1]})}\end{array}

Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

14
.

Case
t=\and{t}{u}
:

We unfold the LHS/RHS first:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket\and{t}{u}\rrbracket(h)&=&\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket u\rrbracket\\
\llparenthesis\and{t}{u}\rrparenthesis(\{h\})&=&\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})\gg\!=\llparenthesis u\rrparenthesis\end{array}

Finally, we have:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket u\rrbracket&\sim&\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})\gg\!=\llparenthesis u\rrparenthesis&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lmm:bind-equiv-wnetkat-gprobnetkat} and IH)}\end{array}

Case
t=\neg t
:

We proceed by a further case analysis on
\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})
.
We have the following two cases (
Smolka et al. 2017
):

(1)

\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\varnothing}
(i.e. the case “false”):

By IH,
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)=0
. We have:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\neg t\rrbracket(h)&=&\eta(h)\\
\llparenthesis\neg t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})&=&\delta_{\{h\}}\end{array}

Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

14
.

(2)

\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\{h\}}
(i.e. the case “true”:)

By IH,
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)=\eta(h)
. We have:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\neg t\rrbracket(h)=0\\
\llparenthesis\neg t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\varnothing}\end{array}

The same reasoning as in the case
t=\mathsf{false}
applies.

∎

Lemma 0 (Conditional branching equivalence across
\mathsf{Prob}
-
wNetKAT

and guarded
ProbNetKAT
).

\begin{array}[]{rrcl}\forall t,p,p^{\prime},q,q^{\prime}\colon&\llbracket p^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}\sim\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}\wedge\llbracket q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}\sim\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis_{n}&\Rightarrow&\llbracket\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p^{\prime}\,\mathsf{else}\,q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}\sim\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q\rrparenthesis_{n}\end{array}

Proof.

We proceed by case analysis on
\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})
. We have
the following two cases (
Smolka et al. 2017
).

(1)

\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\varnothing}
(i.e. “false”)

By
Lemma

19
, we also have
that
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)=0
. Therefore:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p^{\prime}\,\mathsf{else}\,q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}&=&\llbracket p^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:weightings-props})}\\
&\sim&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}&\text{(Assumption)}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q\rrparenthesis_{n}&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Corollary 1]})}\end{array}

(2)

\llparenthesis t\rrparenthesis(\{h\})=\delta_{\{h\}}
(i.e. “true”)

By
Lemma

19
, we also have
that
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)=\eta
. Therefore:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p^{\prime}\,\mathsf{else}\,q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}&=&\llbracket q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{n}&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:weightings-props})}\\
&\sim&\llparenthesis q\rrparenthesis_{n}&\text{(Assumption)}\\
&=&\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,\mathsf{else}\,q\rrparenthesis_{n}&\text{(NetKAT axioms \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{probnetkat}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Corollary 1]})}\end{array}

∎

Lemma 0 (Guarded iteration equivalence across
\mathsf{Prob}
-
wNetKAT

and guarded
ProbNetKAT
).

Given a policy
p
and predicate
t
in the guarded fragment of

ProbNetKAT
:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\colon&\llbracket p\rrbracket_{n}\sim\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis_{n}&\Rightarrow&\llbracket{p}^{(n,t)}\rrbracket_{n}\sim\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis_{n}\end{array}

Proof.

By induction on
n
.

Case
n=0
:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket{p}^{(0,t)}\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\neg t\rrbracket&\text{(By definition)}\\
&\sim&\llparenthesis\neg t\rrparenthesis&\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lmm:predicate-equiv-wnetkat-gprobnetkat})}\\
&=&\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle 0,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

Case
n=n+1
:

We first unfold the LHS/RHS:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket{p}^{(n+1,t)}\rrbracket&=&\llbracket\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,;{p}^{(n,t)}\,\mathsf{else}\,\mathsf{skip}\rrbracket\\
\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n+1,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&=&\llparenthesis\mathsf{if}\,t\,\mathsf{then}\,p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\,\mathsf{else}\,\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis\end{array}

We have that

\llbracket\mathsf{skip}\rrbracket_{n}\sim\llparenthesis\mathsf{skip}\rrparenthesis_{n}
,
so by
Lemma

20
we need
only show that:

\llbracket p\,;{p}^{(n,t)}\rrbracket\sim\llparenthesis p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis

Unfolding the LHS/RHS:

\begin{array}[]{rclr}\llbracket p\,;{p}^{(n,t)}\rrbracket(h)&=&\llbracket p\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket{p}^{(n,t)}\rrbracket&\text{(By definition)}\\
\llparenthesis p\,;{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis(\{h\})&=&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis(\{h\})\gg\!=\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis&\text{(By definition)}\end{array}

By
Lemma

16
, we need only show:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}(1)&\llbracket p\rrbracket(h)&\sim&\llparenthesis p\rrparenthesis(\{h\})\\
(2)&\llbracket{p}^{(n,t)}\rrbracket&\sim&\llparenthesis{p}^{\langle n,t\rangle}\rrparenthesis\end{array}

The first condition holds by assumption for
p
, the second condition
holds by IH for
n
.
∎

Appendix E
Language model

E.1.
Soundness of Reduction to Reduced
wNetKAT
Syntax

We define the reduction map from
wNetKAT
policies to reduced
wNetKAT
policies (
Figure

7
) as in the following figure.

\boldsymbol{p\in\mathsf{Pol}}

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{{\downarrow}(p)\in\mathsf{Pol}^{\downarrow}}}

t

\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathsf{Pk}\end{subarray}}[\pi?\leq t]\odot\pi?

f\leftarrow n

\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathsf{Pk}\end{subarray}}\pi?\,;(\pi[f\coloneq n])!

\mathsf{dup}

\mathsf{dup}

p_{1}\,;p_{2}

{\downarrow}(p_{1})\,;{\downarrow}(p_{2})

r\odot p_{1}

r\odot{\downarrow}{(p_{1})}

p_{1}\oplus p_{2}

{\downarrow}(p_{1})\oplus{\downarrow}(p_{2})

p_{1}^{*}

{\downarrow}(p_{1})^{*}

Figure 12
.
Reduction map for
wNetKAT
policies to reduced
wNetKAT
policies.

Importantly, we have that every
wNetKAT
policy is semantically equivalent to its reduced
counterpart.

Lemma 0 (Soundness of Reduction).

For all
h\in\mathsf{H}
and
p\in\mathsf{Pol}
, we have that
\llbracket p\rrbracket(h)=\llbracket{\downarrow}(p)\rrbracket(h)
, where
{\downarrow}(p)\in\mathsf{Pol}^{\downarrow}
is the reduced policy as defined in
Figure

12
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
p
, for any
h\in\mathsf{H}
.
Base cases:

(
Lemma

5
)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(t)\rrbracket(h)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)

(by definition)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(\mathsf{dup})\rrbracket(h)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(h)

(def of
{\downarrow}(f\leftarrow n)
)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(f\leftarrow n)\rrbracket(h)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}\alpha?\,;(\alpha[f\coloneq n])!\rrbracket(h)

(def of
\llbracket p\oplus q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha?\,;(\alpha[f\coloneq n])!\rrbracket(h)

(def of
\llbracket p\,;q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket(\alpha[f\coloneq n])!\rrbracket(h)

(def of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\lambda h^{\prime\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket(\alpha[f\coloneq n])!\rrbracket(h^{\prime})(h^{\prime\prime})

(lambda equivalence)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket(\alpha[f\coloneq n])!\rrbracket(h^{\prime})(h^{\prime\prime})

(expansion of
h,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket(\alpha[f\coloneq n])!\rrbracket(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime})

(
Propositions

2
and
3
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi=\pi^{\prime}=\alpha\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}=\alpha[f\coloneq n]]

(singleton complete test support)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\{(\pi{::}\hbar)\}}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi=\alpha\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}=\alpha[f\coloneq n]]

(singleton sum)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi=\alpha\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}=\alpha[f\coloneq n]]

(propositional equivalence)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}=\pi[f\coloneq n]]]

(semantics of
f\leftarrow n
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket f\leftarrow n\rrbracket(h)

Inductive cases:

(by definition)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(p\oplus q)\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\downarrow}(p)\oplus{\downarrow}(q)\rrbracket

(by IH on
p
and
q
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket p\oplus q\rrbracket

(by definition)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(p\,;q)\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\downarrow}(p)\,;{\downarrow}(q)\rrbracket

(by IH on
p
and
q
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket p\,;q\rrbracket

(by definition)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(r\odot p)\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket r\odot{\downarrow}(p)\rrbracket

(by IH on
p
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket r\odot p\rrbracket

(by definition)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\downarrow}(p^{*})\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\downarrow}(p)^{*}\rrbracket

(by IH on
p
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket p^{*}\rrbracket

∎

E.2.
Lemmas for Soundness of Reduction to Reduced
wNetKAT
Syntax

Proposition 2.

Every complete test
\pi?\triangleq f_{1}=n_{1}\,;\dots\,;f_{k}=n_{k}
matches exactly one packet:

\llbracket\pi?\rrbracket(\alpha{::}\hbar)(\beta{::}\hbar^{\prime})=[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\alpha=\beta=\{f_{1}=n_{1},\dots,f_{k}=n_{k}\}]

Proposition 3.

Every complete assignment
\pi!\triangleq f_{1}\leftarrow n_{1}\,;\dots\,;f_{k}\leftarrow n_{k}
matches exactly one packet:

\llbracket\pi!\rrbracket(\alpha{::}\hbar)(\beta{::}\hbar^{\prime})=[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\beta=\{f_{1}=n_{1},\dots,f_{k}=n_{k}\}]

In the following lemma (and accompanying proof) we adopt the convention of denoting
complete tests by
\alpha
,
\beta
, and
\gamma
, whereas we
denote complete assignments by
\pi
. We write

\pi_{\alpha}
,
\pi_{\beta}
, etc., to emphasize a complete
assignment to the packet
\alpha
.

Lemma 0.

For all complete tests
\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}
and complete assignments
\pi_{\beta}\in\mathsf{Pk}^{!}
:

(1)

\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket=\llbracket\alpha\,;\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket

(2)

\llbracket\alpha\,;\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\,;\alpha\rrbracket

(3)

\llbracket\mathsf{true}\rrbracket=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}\alpha\rrbracket

(4)

\llbracket\pi_{\beta}\rrbracket=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}\alpha\,;\pi_{\beta}\rrbracket

(5)

\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}\alpha\,;\pi_{\alpha}\,;\mathsf{dup}\,;\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket

Proof.

For all histories
h\in\mathsf{H}
:

(definition of
\llbracket p\,;q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\alpha\,;\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket(h)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket(h^{\prime})(h^{\prime\prime})

(expansion of
h,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime})

(
Propositions

2
and
3
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi]\cdot{[\hbar^{\prime}=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]}

(Iverson bracket multiplication)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}{[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]}

(By
Proposition

2
:
\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))=\{\pi_{0}{::}\hbar\mid\pi_{0}=\pi_{\alpha}\}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;{[\hbar=\hbar=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]}

(predicate simplification)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;{[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]}

(
Proposition

2
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime})

(implicit lambda)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar)

(
h=\pi_{0}{::}\hbar
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)

(definition of
\llbracket p\,;q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\alpha\,;\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(h)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(h^{\prime})(h^{\prime\prime})

(expansion of
h,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime})

(
Propositions

2
and
3
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi]\cdot{[\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}]}

(Iverson bracket multiplication)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]

(By
Proposition

2
:
\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))=\{\pi_{0}{::}\hbar\mid\pi_{0}=\pi_{\alpha}\}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;[\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar\wedge\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]

(predicate simplification)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;[\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]

(By def. of
\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket
:
|\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))|=1
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]

(By def. of
\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket
:
\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))=\{\pi_{0}{::}\pi_{0}{::}\hbar\}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar^{\prime\prime}=\hbar^{\prime}=\pi_{0}{::}\hbar\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi]

(Iverson bracket multiplication)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\hbar^{\prime}=\pi_{0}{::}\hbar]\cdot{[\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\pi\wedge\hbar^{\prime}=\hbar^{\prime\prime}]}

(By
Proposition

2
and def. of
\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime})

(definition of
h,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(h))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h^{\prime})(h^{\prime\prime})

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\rrbracket(h)\gg\!=\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket

(definition of
\llbracket p\,;q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathsf{dup}\,;\alpha\rrbracket(h)

(definition of
\llbracket p\oplus q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}\alpha\rrbracket(h)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)

(explicit lambda)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)\big)(h^{\prime})

(definition of lifted
+
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})

(expansion of
h,h^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi_{0}{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime})

(
Proposition

2
, for packet
\pi
corresponding to
\alpha
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}=\pi]

(
\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\cong\mathsf{Pk}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}).\;[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}\wedge\pi_{0}=\pi^{\prime}_{0}]

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}).\;[\pi_{0}{::}\hbar=\pi^{\prime}_{0}{::}\hbar^{\prime}]

(definition of
h,h^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;[h=h^{\prime}]

(definition of

)

\llbracket p\,;q\rrbracket
)

)

(lambda equivalence)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\pi_{\beta}\rrbracket(h^{\prime})(h^{\prime\prime})

)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar)(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\cdot\llbracket\pi_{\beta}\rrbracket(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime})

(
Propositions

and

)

(Iverson bracket multiplication)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi=\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{\alpha}\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{\beta}]

)

\displaystyle=\lambda(\pi^{\prime\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime\prime}).\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(\pi^{\prime}{::}\hbar^{\prime})\in\mathsf{supp}(\llbracket\alpha\rrbracket(\pi{::}\hbar))}\end{subarray}}[\hbar=\hbar^{\prime}=\hbar^{\prime\prime}\wedge\pi=\pi^{\prime}\wedge\pi^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{\beta}]

(for unique

(

(

)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\pi_{\beta}\rrbracket(h)

(by monadic structure of

(

, of

\displaystyle=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}\alpha\,;\pi_{\alpha}\,;\mathsf{dup}\,;\pi_{\alpha}\rrbracket

Lemma 0.

For all
t\in\mathsf{Pred}
:
\llbracket t\rrbracket=\llbracket{\downarrow}(t)\rrbracket

Proof.

Fix two given histories
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}
. Then, as
(\mathsf{Pk}^{?},\,\vee\,,\and{}{},\neg,\mathsf{false},\mathsf{true})
forms a Boolean algebra we have that
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})
.

We now show that
\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})
. We proceed by case analysis.

Suppose that
\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}
. Then:

(
A\vee B=\bot\implies A=\bot\wedge B=\bot
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;\alpha?\leq t\Rightarrow\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

(
A\Rightarrow B=\bot\implies A\wedge B=\bot
)

\displaystyle\implies\forall\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;\alpha?\leq t\wedge\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

(Iverson bracket multiplication)

\displaystyle\implies\forall\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;[\alpha?\leq t]\cdot\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

(def of
\llbracket r\odot p\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\implies\forall\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;\llbracket[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

(
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{0}=\mathbb{0}
)

\displaystyle\implies\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

(def of
\llbracket p\oplus q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\implies\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

Suppose that
\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}
. Then:

(
A\vee B=\top\implies A=\top\vee B=\top
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

\displaystyle\implies\exists\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;\alpha?\leq t\wedge\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

(
\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\cong\mathsf{Pk}
, for
h=\pi{::}\hbar
, only
\llbracket\alpha?_{\pi}\rrbracket(h)(h)=\mathbb{1}
)

\displaystyle\implies\exists!\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;\alpha?\leq t\wedge\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

(Iverson bracket multiplication)

\displaystyle\implies\exists!\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;[\alpha?\leq t]\cdot\llbracket\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

(def of
\llbracket r\odot p\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\implies\exists!\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}.\;\llbracket[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

(singleton supported index set)

\displaystyle\implies\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}}\end{subarray}}\llbracket[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

(def of
\llbracket p\oplus q\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle\implies\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

As
t\in\mathsf{Pred}
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\in\{\mathbb{0},\mathbb{1}\}
, and so
\llbracket t\rrbracket=\llbracket\bigvee_{\alpha?\leq t}\alpha?\rrbracket=\llbracket\BigAdd\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha?\in\mathsf{Pk}^{?}\end{subarray}}[\alpha?\leq t]\odot\alpha?\rrbracket=\llbracket{\downarrow}(t)\rrbracket
.

∎

E.3.
Properties of the Language Model

Lemma 0.

For all
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}
,
p\in\mathsf{Pol}
,
r\in\mathcal{S}
:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))}\end{subarray}}G(r\odot p)(x)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(r\odot p))}\end{subarray}}G(r\odot p)(x)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})

Proof.

For convenience, let
J,K\subseteq\mathsf{GS}
and
I\colon\mathsf{GS}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
be given by:

J\triangleq\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\qquad K\triangleq\mathsf{supp}(G(r\odot p))\qquad I(x)=G(r\odot p)(x)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})

We will first consider the sums over sets
J^{\prime}\subseteq J
and
K^{\prime}\subseteq K
of indices leading to non-zero terms:

J^{\prime}\triangleq\{x\mid I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in J\}\qquad K^{\prime}\triangleq\{x\mid I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in K\}

We now show that
J^{\prime}=K^{\prime}
. Note that
J^{\prime}\subseteq K^{\prime}
as:

(definition of
J^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle x\in J^{\prime}

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in J

(
A\wedge B\implies A
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(
A\implies A\wedge A
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
I(x)
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(r\odot p)(x)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\neq\mathbb{0}

(
\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\in\{\mathbb{0},\mathbb{1}\}
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(r\odot p)(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}

(
A\wedge B\implies A
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(r\odot p)(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
\mathsf{supp}(G(r\odot p))
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(r\odot p))

(definition of
K
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in K

(definition of
K^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\implies x\in K^{\prime}

And similarly,
K^{\prime}\subseteq J^{\prime}
as:

(definition of
K^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle x\in K^{\prime}

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in K

(definition of
K
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(r\odot p))

(definition of
\mathsf{supp}(G(r\odot p))
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(r\odot p)(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
G(r\odot p)
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge r\cdot G(p)(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(
Definition

7
.
4
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge r\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(p)(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(
A\wedge B\implies A
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(p)(x)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
\mathsf{supp}(G(p))
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))

(definition of
J
)

\displaystyle\implies I(x)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in J

(definition of
J^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\implies x\in J^{\prime}

Therefore
J^{\prime}=K^{\prime}
and so
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in J^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}I(x)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in K^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}I(x)
. Finally, by the properties of
\omega
-continuous monoids:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in J}\end{subarray}}I(x)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in J^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}I(x)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in K^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}I(x)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in K}\end{subarray}}I(x)

∎

Lemma 0.

For all
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}
and
p,q\in\mathsf{Pol}
, the following sums are equal:

\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))}\end{subarray}}G(p)(x)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\big)+\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in\mathsf{supp}(G(q))}\end{subarray}}G(q)(y)\cdot\llbracket y\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\big)

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{z\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\cup\mathsf{supp}(G(q))}\end{subarray}}(G(p)(z)\cdot\llbracket z\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime}))+(G(q)(z)\cdot\llbracket z\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime}))

Proof.

Let
I_{1},I_{2}\colon\mathsf{GS}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
defined by
I_{1}(g)=G(p)(g)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})
and
I_{2}(g)=G(q)(g)\cdot\llbracket y\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})
. Additionally, let
X_{1}=\mathsf{supp}(G(p))
and
X_{2}=\mathsf{supp}(G(q))
. Then:

(Definition of
X_{1}
)

\displaystyle g\notin X_{1}

\displaystyle\implies g\notin\mathsf{supp}(G(p))

(Definition of
\mathsf{supp}
)

\displaystyle\implies G(p)(g)=\mathbb{0}

(
Definition

7
.
4
)

\displaystyle\implies G(p)(g)\cdot\llbracket x\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}

(Definition of
I_{1}(g)
)

\displaystyle\implies I_{1}(g)=\mathbb{0}

The reasoning to show that
g\notin X_{2}\implies I_{2}(g)=\mathbb{0}
is analogous. By properties of
\omega
-continuous monoids, we have:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x_{1}\in X_{1}}\end{subarray}}I_{1}(x_{1})+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x_{2}\in X_{2}}\end{subarray}}I_{2}(x_{2})=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X_{1}\cup X_{2}}\end{subarray}}I_{1}(x)+I_{2}(x)

And so, by definition of
X_{1},X_{2}
and
I_{1},I_{2}
, the claim is proved.
∎

Lemma 0.

For all
p,q\in\mathsf{Pol}
,
\mathsf{supp}(G(p\oplus q))=\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\cup\mathsf{supp}(G(q))
.

Proof.

An element is in
\mathsf{supp}(G(p\oplus q))
if and only if it is in
\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\cup\mathsf{supp}(G(q))
:

(definition of
\mathsf{supp}
)

\displaystyle z\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p\oplus q))

\displaystyle\iff G(p\oplus q)(z)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
G(p\oplus q
))

\displaystyle\iff G(p)(z)+G(q)(z)\neq\mathbb{0}

(
\omega
-cont. monoid is zerosumfree)

\displaystyle\iff G(p)(z)\neq\mathbb{0}\vee G(q)(z)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
\mathsf{supp}
)

\displaystyle\iff z\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\vee z\in\mathsf{supp}(G(q))

(property of set union)

\displaystyle\iff z\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\cup\mathsf{supp}(G(q))

Therefore both sets have exactly the same elements, therefore they are equal.
∎

Lemma 0.

For countable sets
X
and
Y
, if
f\colon X\rightarrow\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathsf{H})
and
g\colon Y\rightarrow(\mathsf{H}\rightarrow\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathsf{H}))
then:

\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x)\big)\gg\!=\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}g(y)\big)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(f(x)\gg\!=g(y)\big)

Proof.

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x)\big)\gg\!=\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}g(y)\big)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x))}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x)\big)(h)\cdot\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}g(y)\big)(h)(h^{\prime})

(definition of lifted
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{}\end{subarray}}
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x))}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\big)\cdot\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)

(prop. of
\omega
-cont. monoids)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\big)\cdot\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)

(
Lemma

9
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(
Lemma

9
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(assoc. of countable sum)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(assoc. of countable sum)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(prop. of
\omega
-cont. monoids)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f(x))}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(definition of lifted
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{}\end{subarray}}
)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f(x))}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(definition of lifted
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{}\end{subarray}}
)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f(x))}\end{subarray}}f(x)(h)\cdot g(y)(h)(h^{\prime})\big)\big)

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(f(x)\gg\!=g(y)\big)\big)

(notational equivalence)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in X}\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{y\in Y}\end{subarray}}\big(f(x)\gg\!=g(y)\big)

∎

Lemma 0.

If
f\colon\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathsf{H})
,
g\colon\mathsf{H}\rightarrow\mathbfcal{W}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathsf{H})
, and
r\in\mathcal{S}
then:

f\gg\!=\big(\lambda h.r\cdot g(h)\big)~{}={}~(f\cdot r)\gg\!=g

Proof.

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle f\gg\!=\big(\lambda h.r\cdot g(h)\big)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f)}\end{subarray}}f(h)\cdot\big(\lambda h.\;r\cdot g(h)\big)(h)(h^{\prime})

(
\lambda
application)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f)}\end{subarray}}f(h)\cdot\big(r\cdot g(h)\big)(h^{\prime})

(definition of lifted
\cdot
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f)}\end{subarray}}f(h)\cdot\big(r\cdot g(h)(h^{\prime})\big)

(prop. of
\omega
-cont. monoids)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}f(h)\cdot\big(r\cdot g(h)(h^{\prime})\big)

(associativity of
\cdot
)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{H}}\end{subarray}}\big(f(h)\cdot r\big)\cdot g(h)(h^{\prime})

(prop. of
\omega
-cont. monoids)

\displaystyle=\lambda h^{\prime}.\;\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{h\in\mathsf{supp}(f\cdot r)}\end{subarray}}\big(f(h)\cdot r\big)\cdot g(h)(h^{\prime})

(definition of
\gg\!=
)

\displaystyle=(f\cdot r)\gg\!=g

∎

Lemma 0.

\mathsf{supp}(G(p\,;q))=\{x\diamond y\mid G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\wedge y\in\mathsf{supp}(G(q))\}

Proof.

We show that the sets have the same elements, and must then be equal.

(definition of
\mathsf{supp}(G(p\,;q))
)

\displaystyle z\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p\,;q))

\displaystyle\iff G(p\,;q)(z)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
G(p\,;q)
)

\displaystyle\iff(G(p)\diamond G(q))(z)\neq\mathbb{0}

(definition of
(G(p)\diamond G(q))
)

\displaystyle\iff\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p)),y\in\mathsf{supp}(G(q)),z=x\diamond y}\end{subarray}}G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}

(for some
x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p)),y\in\mathsf{supp}(G(q))
, by
\omega
-cont. monoids being zerosumfree)

\displaystyle\iff z=x\diamond y\wedge G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}

(by set condition)

\displaystyle\iff z\in\{x\diamond y\mid G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge x\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\wedge y\in\mathsf{supp}(G(q))\}

∎

Lemma 0.

If
p,q\in\mathsf{Pol}
, then:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(x,y)\in I}\end{subarray}}S(x,y)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{j\in J}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(x,y)\in K_{j}}\end{subarray}}S(x,y)\big)

where:

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{supp}(G(p\,;q))

\displaystyle K_{j}

\displaystyle\triangleq\{(x,y)\mid(x,y)\in I\wedge x\diamond y=j\}

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{supp}(G(p))\times\mathsf{supp}(G(q))

\displaystyle S(x,y)

\displaystyle\triangleq G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\cdot\llbracket x\diamond y\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})

Proof.

We first consider sums over sets
I^{\prime}\subseteq I
and
K^{\prime}\subseteq K
of indices leading to non-zero terms:

I^{\prime}=\{(x,y)\mid S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in I\}\qquad K_{j}^{\prime}=\{(x,y)\mid S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in K_{j}\}

We now show that
I^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in J}K^{\prime}_{j}
. Note that
I^{\prime}\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}K^{\prime}_{j}
as:

(definition of
I^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle(x,y)\in I^{\prime}

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in I

(
A\implies A\wedge A
)

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in I

(definition of
S(x,y)
)

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\cdot\llbracket x\diamond y\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in I

(
\llbracket x\diamond y\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})\in\{\mathbb{0},\mathbb{1}\}
)

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge\llbracket x\diamond y\rrbracket(h)(h^{\prime})=\mathbb{1}\wedge(x,y)\in I

(by definition of
K_{(x\diamond y)}
, as
x\diamond y
is defined)

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in K_{(x\diamond y)}

(definition of
K^{\prime}_{(x\diamond y)}
)

\displaystyle\implies G(p)(x)\cdot G(q)(y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in K^{\prime}_{(x\diamond y)}

(
Lemma

11
)

\displaystyle\implies(x\diamond y)\in\mathsf{supp}(G(p\,;q))\wedge(x,y)\in K^{\prime}_{(x\diamond y)}

(definition of
J
)

\displaystyle\implies(x\diamond y)\in J\wedge(x,y)\in K^{\prime}_{(x\diamond y)}

(when
j=x\diamond y
)

\displaystyle\implies(x,y)\in\bigcup_{j\in J}K^{\prime}_{j}

Additionally,
\bigcup_{j\in J}K^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq I^{\prime}
as:

(for some
j\in J
)

\displaystyle(x,y)\in\bigcup_{j\in J}K^{\prime}_{j}

\displaystyle\implies(x,y)\in K^{\prime}_{j}\wedge j\in J

(
A\wedge B\implies A
)

\displaystyle\implies(x,y)\in K^{\prime}_{j}

(definition of
K^{\prime}_{j}
)

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in K_{j}

(definition of
K_{j}
)

\displaystyle\implies S(x,y)\neq\mathbb{0}\wedge(x,y)\in I

(definition of
I^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\implies(x,y)\in I^{\prime}

Therefore,
I^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in J}K^{\prime}_{j}
. Furthermore, note that for any
i,j\in J
, if
i\neq j
, then:

(definition of
K_{i}
,
K_{j}
)

\displaystyle K_{i}\cap K_{j}

\displaystyle=\{(x,y)\mid(x,y)\in I\wedge x\diamond y=i\}\cap\{(x,y)\mid(x,y)\in I\wedge x\diamond y=j\}

(
\wedge
of set conditions)

\displaystyle=\{(x,y)\mid(x,y)\in I\wedge x\diamond y=i\wedge x\diamond y=j\}

(transitivity of equality)

\displaystyle=\{(x,y)\mid(x,y)\in I\wedge i=x\diamond y=j\}

(as
i\neq j
)

\displaystyle=\varnothing

And as
K^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq K_{i}
, then
I^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in J}K_{j}^{\prime}=\biguplus_{j\in J}K_{j}^{\prime}
. Thus, by properties of
\omega
-continuous monoids, we have:

\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(x,y)\in I}\end{subarray}}S(x,y)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(x,y)\in I^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}S(x,y)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{j\in J}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(x,y)\in K_{j}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}S(x,y)\big)~{}={}~\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{j\in J}\end{subarray}}\big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(x,y)\in K_{j}}\end{subarray}}S(x,y)\big)

∎
